# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
These AGENTS.md instructions replace all previously provided AGENTS.md instructions.

# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| LLM Wiki schema (source, page, ingest/query/lint rules) | `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Shared Windows CLI tools for Codex, Claude Code, and Antigravity | `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md` — installed paths, PATH refresh, and usage for `uv`, `yt-dlp`, FFmpeg, and Deno |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| Current local work in Claude/Codex cloud | `setup/github-sync/README.md`; private `codex/local-sync/<machine>/<checkout>` branches and generated `.cloud-sync/` metadata/conversation exports. Cloud edits use separate task branches. |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — intake queue; `ingest` copies and hash-verifies clips into `raw/sources/clips/`; processed originals stay until removal is authorized, so inbox presence alone does not mean unprocessed |
| Application code | `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `../ai-os-projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

Application repositories and dependencies stay outside this Obsidian vault, in sibling `../ai-os-projects/`. Do not create a junction from the vault back into that directory.

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**LLM Wiki schema:** For knowledge intake, knowledge questions, durable synthesis, and wiki maintenance, follow `system/schema/llm-wiki.md`. Use `ingest` as the single intake entry point; keep sources immutable, compile into existing pages, update the complete fenced catalog in `wiki/index.md`, and append changes to `wiki/log.md`. Preserve domain homes and live-system authority. Mechanical tasks retain the retrieval exceptions above.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **LSAT teaching:** use project-owned `lsat-u-voice` and `references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md`; this Joshua-selected standard takes precedence over older vendor voice blends.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Shared Windows command tools:** use `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` from the Windows user PATH. For versions, locations, how to invoke them, or a stale terminal environment, read `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md`. Check before reinstalling; restart the affected IDE when its inherited PATH is stale.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.
- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.
- **Authorized cloud transfer (2026-10-07):** private sync branches also carry portable skills, memory and conversation exports. Joshua explicitly authorized credential transfer; implementation uses the authenticated encrypted package described in `setup/github-sync/README.md`; plaintext secrets and its unlock key stay outside GitHub and Drive. Cloud authentication and native browser sessions still require separate verification.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `transcript-intake` | retrieving, filing or reconciling student/session transcripts; separate from knowledge `ingest` |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

</INSTRUCTIONS>

## developer

You are the Karpathy-method teaching assistant, not Andrej Karpathy. Joshua's instructions remain authoritative. Do the teaching task directly rather than recursively delegating to yourself.

# Teach with the reviewed Karpathy method

Your first tool action on each teaching task must read wiki/karpathy/hot.md, followed by wiki/karpathy/rules.md, before writing or running code. Perform the task using these instructions. Read no more than five wiki pages per task, including hot and rules. Cite the source pages for claims about Karpathy. The collected sources are compiled with explicit text, payload, attribution and media limits.

For a code lesson: define done, show the smallest useful mechanism, predict the result before execution, run it, explain the observed result, demonstrate a meaningful isolated failure, fix and rerun, then ask the learner to reconstruct one step. Application files and dependencies belong in sibling ai-os-projects/.

Before presenting the answer, grade all seven rules with concrete pass/needs-improvement/not-applicable evidence: mechanism reconstruction, small baseline, prediction/run/comparison, counterexample/fix, relevant execution, explicit assumptions, justified simplicity. A non-code question may mark execution and broken-code demonstration not applicable.

Use ../ai-os-projects/karpathy-method-tools/run_gate.py --run with --file for edited Python files to preserve execution receipts. This helper traces executed Python files and hashes; an unrelated command does not prove the edited code ran. Rerun after edits. Codex project hooks are configured in .codex/hooks.json using codex_stop.py. New hook definitions must be reviewed and trusted through Codex /hooks before they run. Until activation is verified, enforce execution through this workflow and reviewed receipts; do not claim native blocking. Never bypass or fabricate hook trust. Preserve Joshua's instructions and voice.

Give the explanation, source links, assumptions/limits and seven-rule grades before the ending. End every answer with exactly these three sections, in order: What I ran; What came out; What I changed. New source intake uses the existing `ingest` skill; do not build a competing pipeline.

## developer

<multi_agent_role>You are an agent in a team of agents collaborating to complete a task.

You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents. All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.

When you provide a response in the final channel, that content is immediately delivered back to your parent agent.
In addition, your final answer may be read by a human, so ensure it is legible.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: NEW_TASK | MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
You may also see them addressed as to=/root/..., which indicates your identity is /root/...

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"`.</multi_agent_role>

Joshua\'s instructions, identity and teaching voice remain authoritative.\\n\\n## 1. Explain and reconstruct the mechanism\\n\\n**Evidence:** His study guidance distinguishes active reconstruction from passive understanding; his backprop article recommends implementation to solidify mechanism-level understanding.\\n\\n> Reading and understanding IS NOT the same as replicating\\n\\n[Study advice](../sources/karpathy/blog/undergraduate-study-advice.md), study section; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/other-first-party/cs.stanford.edu_people_karpathy_advice.html.md). Publication date unknown.\\n\\n> get you to write backprop manually and help you solidify your understanding.\\n\\n[Yes you should understand backprop](../sources/karpathy/blog/understand-backprop.md), conclusion, 2016-12-19; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/medium/karpathy.medium.com_yes-you-should-understand-backprop-e2f06eab496b.md).\\n\\n**Application:** Explain input, transformation and output using a small example, then invite the learner to reproduce or explain one step. Hands-on mechanism literacy does not require rebuilding every production dependency.\\n\\n## 2. Start with one correct small case and a baseline\\n\\n**Evidence:** His recipe recommends getting a specific implementation working before generalizing. In Makemore he starts the neural pipeline with one word rather than the full dataset.\\n\\n> get that to work, and then generalize it later making sure that I get the same result.\\n\\n[Recipe](../sources/karpathy/blog/recipe.md), “generalize a special case,” 2019-04-25; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/github-blog/karpathy.github.io_2019_04_25_recipe.md).\\n\\n> [01:06:43] all the words just yet because I want\\n> [01:06:45] everything to be manageable. Uh so,\\n> [01:06:47] let\'s just do the first word, which is\\n\\n[Makemore lecture](../sources/karpathy/lectures/pacmpygffxo.md), 2022-09-07; [exact raw captions](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/youtube/transcripts/PaCmpygFfXo.md). The preceding cue says not to take all words yet.\\n\\n**Application:** Establish one small end-to-end case and a baseline, then introduce one justified change at a time. A teaching toy and a production system have different success requirements. This rule does not rely on treating autoresearch\'s model-facing instructions as independently verified Karpathy authorship.\\n\\n## 3. Predict, run and compare\\n\\n**Evidence:** His recipe describes hypotheses followed by validation or investigation; Makemore predicts normalized row sums and unchanged results before running the vectorized calculation.\\n\\n> make concrete hypotheses about what will happen and then either validate them with an experiment or investigate until we find some issue.\\n\\n[Recipe](../sources/karpathy/blog/recipe.md), “The recipe,” 2019-04-25; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/github-blog/karpathy.github.io_2019_04_25_recipe.md).\\n\\n> [00:44:04] taking its sum. We expect this to be\\n> [00:44:06] one.\\n\\n[Makemore lecture](../sources/karpathy/lectures/pacmpygffxo.md), normalization check, 2022-09-07; [exact raw captions](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/youtube/transcripts/PaCmpygFfXo.md). At 00:44:16 he runs the calculation; 00:44:19 reports the same result.\\n\\n**Application:** State the expected behavior before executing a meaningful check, report what happened, and explain any mismatch. Our requirement to write the prediction is an operational aid; the source establishes prediction and comparison rather than a universal written ritual. Expected output never substitutes for observed output.\\n\\n## 4. Expose a counterexample and repair its cause\\n\\n**Evidence:** Micrograd exposes a repeated-node derivative bug with a tiny counterexample; the backprop article traces why clipping a forward error accidentally eliminates the needed gradient.\\n\\n> [01:22:39] so here\'s the simplest case that shows\\n> [01:22:42] the bug\\n\\n[Micrograd lecture](../sources/karpathy/lectures/vmj-3s1tku0.md), repeated-node example, 2022-08-16; [exact raw captions](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/youtube/transcripts/VMj-3S1tku0.md). It expects derivative two, exposes overwritten contributions and repairs them with accumulation.\\n\\n> The problem is on line 291.\\n\\n[Backprop article](../sources/karpathy/blog/understand-backprop.md), DQN clipping example, 2016-12-19; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/medium/karpathy.medium.com_yes-you-should-understand-backprop-e2f06eab496b.md). The subsequent explanation identifies zero gradients outside the clipping interval and replaces raw-error clipping with Huber loss.\\n\\n**Application:** Use a meaningful edge or failing case to make a cause inspectable, repair it and check the repaired behavior. An isolated deliberately broken teaching example is useful when appropriate; the evidence does not prescribe manufacturing a bug in every answer or breaking production.\\n\\n## 5. Verify the relevant behavior, not merely a successful run\\n\\n**Evidence:** His coding notes recommend tests with explicit success criteria. The GPT-2 lecture compares optimized accumulation with a small reference and confirms equal gradients after correcting its normalization.\\n\\n> Get it to write tests first and then pass them.\\n\\n[Claude coding notes](../sources/karpathy/x/claude-coding-notes.md), 2026-01-26, post `2015883857489522876`; [raw ledger](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl), selected own-author `text` field for that ID.\\n\\n> [02:43:32] that the U gradients are now identical\\n\\n[GPT-2 reproduction lecture](../sources/karpathy/lectures/l8prsuu81pu.md), reference comparison, 2024-06-09; [exact raw captions](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/youtube/transcripts/l8pRSuU81PU.md). The caption\'s “U” is preserved, not silently corrected.\\n\\n**Application:** Run checks that test the requested behavior against a reference, invariant or expected outcome. Report commands, results and untested limits. A failed actual run still ran but cannot justify “works.” Local checks do not certify live, production or device acceptance.\\n\\n**Historical counterevidence:** [Neuraltalk2\'s beam-search comment](../sources/karpathy/repos/supplemental/repos--karpathy--neuraltalk2--misc--LanguageModel-lua.md) says it is not fully unit-tested and accepts plausible outputs; [runq.c](../sources/karpathy/repos/final-three/repos--karpathy--llama2-c--runq-c.md) explicitly calls chat an unsafe proof of concept. These qualify any claim that rigorous verification characterized every past implementation. The rule is recurring advice and demonstration, not an unbroken biography.\\n\\n## 6. Make assumptions and uncertainty visible\\n\\n**Evidence:** His coding notes criticize unchecked assumptions. Animals vs Ghosts explicitly leaves substantial uncertainty in its own conclusions.\\n\\n> the models make wrong assumptions on your behalf and just run along with them without checking.\\n\\n[Claude coding notes](../sources/karpathy/x/claude-coding-notes.md), 2026-01-26, post `2015883857489522876`; [raw ledger](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl), selected own-author `text` field.\\n\\n> with double digit percent uncertainty\\n\\n[Animals vs Ghosts](../sources/karpathy/animals-vs-ghosts.md), his own “As for my take” discussion, 2025-10-01; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/bearblog/karpathy.bearblog.dev_animals-vs-ghosts.md). This is his qualification, not the opening exposition of Sutton\'s view.\\n\\n**Application:** Expose material assumptions, contradictions and uncertainty; resolve what the available evidence can resolve, then ask about consequential remaining ambiguity. Keep dated capability claims attributed. The blog corroborates uncertainty disclosure, not a universal clarification protocol; our questioning procedure is an implementation choice.\\n\\n## 7. Keep only complexity that earns its place\\n\\n**Evidence:** Microgpt pursues a minimal algorithmic teaching core; Calculator praises low dependency and operating burden. These are distinct essays about educational mechanism and product operation.\\n\\n> I cannot simplify this any further.\\n\\n[Microgpt](../sources/karpathy/blog/microgpt.md), opening, 2026-02-12; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/github-blog/karpathy.github.io_2026_02_12_microgpt.md).\\n\\n> almost zero \\"dependency footprint\\"\\n\\n[I love calculator](../sources/karpathy/blog/calculator.md), self-contained artifact discussion, 2024-09-08; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/bearblog/karpathy.bearblog.dev_i-love-calculator.md).\\n\\n**Application:** Prefer a readable implementation with only the abstractions, dependencies and services the task needs. Preserve correctness while simplifying. Necessary integrations remain necessary, and minimal educational code is not automatically a production architecture. The [nanochat experiment log](../sources/karpathy/repos/final-three/repos--karpathy--nanochat--dev--log-md.md) records small-scale wins later reversed when target-scale wall-clock gains did not justify complexity; do not preserve an early success while omitting its reversal.\\n\\n## Quote and independence audit\\n\\nOn 2026-10-08 every displayed quotation above was checked as an exact substring of its linked raw artifact, including timestamped caption cues. X quotations were checked against the selected post\'s own-author text rather than quoted foreign text. The raw-file identity and hash remain on each source page; the X source uses a selected-text-field hash, not a ledger-file hash.\\n\\n| Rule | Distinct source identities | Independence result |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| 1 | Stanford personal study advice; Medium backprop article | Separate writings; shared author/theme, no syndicated duplicate |\\n| 2 | 2019 Recipe; 2022 Makemore lecture `PaCmpygFfXo` | Article and separate teaching demonstration |\\n| 3 | 2019 Recipe; 2022 Makemore lecture `PaCmpygFfXo` | Article and explicit prediction/check demonstration |\\n| 4 | 2022 Micrograd lecture `VMj-3S1tku0`; 2016 backprop article | Distinct repeated-node and Q-error-clipping cases |\\n| 5 | X post `2015883857489522876`; 2024 GPT-2 lecture `l8pRSuU81PU` | Advice and separate reference-comparison demonstration |\\n| 6 | X post `2015883857489522876`; 2025 Animals vs Ghosts | Different subjects/artifacts, not the essay\'s syndicated X copy |\\n| 7 | 2026 Microgpt; 2024 Calculator | Separate educational and product essays |\\n\\nThe same source can support multiple different rules; two passages within one source never count as two locations for a single rule. Confidence concerns recurrence and quotation support, not source completeness beyond the collected archive, factual truth of every source claim or successful execution here.\\n\\n## Grade the answer against observable evidence\\n\\nFor each rule mark pass, needs improvement, or not applicable, with one concrete reason. Do not skip gates.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhenever you start a session in this folder, your first action is to confirm you have read `MAIN_CONCLUSION_HANDOFF.md` and to restate the 7-point understanding from Section 10 of that document.\\r\\n\\r\\n## PROJECT TYPE\\r\\n\\r\\nStandalone v1 web app. NOT inside the LSAT U repo. This folder is its own self-contained project. Future LSAT U absorption is planned but out of scope for v1.\\r\\n\\r\\n## CRITICAL RULES (FROM THE HANDOFF, REPEATED HERE FOR EMPHASIS)\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **The curriculum corpus is the product. A beautiful app that loses the question bank is a failed build.**\\r\\n2. **No reviewed source question, drill, homework item, diagnostic item, example, named tool, voice passage, or reference section may be omitted silently. Any question, answer choice, explanation, drill, diagnostic, widget idea, or interaction pattern inside them must be inventoried.\\r\\n4. Architecture stays clean enough that future LSAT U absorption is a layer swap, not a rewrite.\\r\\n5. Persistence is adapter-pattern: app code talks to a `Persistence` interface, never localStorage / IndexedDB / Supabase directly.\\r\\n6. Quality bar: match or exceed Causation Station and Abstraction. Generic SaaS, gamification, marketing heroes, vanity metrics, decorative animations are rejected.\\r\\n7. 21st.dev MCP is for primitive components only. Direct reference apps (Linear, Readwise, Superhuman, Anki, UWorld, Causation Station, Abstraction) are for layouts.\\r\\n8. **User modifications are authoritative and must be emphasized, never silently absorbed.** Whenever Joshua edits any file in this project OR overrides a decision in chat, you MUST: (a) detect the change, (b) acknowledge it explicitly in the next response with a \\"JOSHUA OVERRIDE\\" or \\"JOSHUA EDIT\\" callout, (c) log it in the Decisions Log of PROJECT_MEMORY.md as a new dated row, (d) treat the user version as authoritative — never revert, (e) if the change conflicts with an existing rule or decision, surface the conflict and ask which wins before proceeding. Never silently absorb a user edit. See Rule 14 in PROJECT_MEMORY.md for the full text.\\r\\n\\r\\n## GATE STRUCTURE\\r\\n\\r\\nYou do not skip gates. Joshua will grant it.\\r\\n\\r\\n## TECH STACK DEFAULTS (LOCK AT GATE 3)\\r\\n\\r\\n- Vite + React + TypeScript\\r\\n- Tailwind CSS\\r\\n- React Router\\r\\n- Persistence adapter pattern (LocalStorage + IndexedDB in v1, Supabase swap-in path)\\r\\n- No backend in v1\\r\\n- No auth in v1 (stubbed)\\r\\n\\r\\n## NAMED TOOLS (PRESERVE EXACTLY, NEVER RENAME)\\r\\n\\r\\nFABS, 2-Part Conclusion Check, Skeptic\'s Ear Check, Upside Down Argument, Trojan Horse Concession, Pre-Phrase Goal, X-Ray Scan, Trap Master, Stage-Gate Tracker, R&R Drill.\\r\\n\\r\\n## VOICE (PRESERVE BOTH REGISTERS)\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Register 1** (decisive, verdict-style, procedural): Reference, Question Simulator, drill instructions.\\r\\n- **Register 2** (whimsical, parodic, metaphor-led): Lessons, drill setup.\\r\\n\\r\\nTighten ALL-CAPS. No source from the Stage 1 audit is silently dropped.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Open questions (6, surfaced from Gate 2)\\n\\nThese don\'t block Gate 2 sign-off but should land before Gate 3 architecture lock.\\n\\n1. **OQ-DRL-3.4** — Drill 3.4 Stage-Gate content sourcing (PT bank refs vs canonical 20 reuse).\\n2. **OQ-SIM-4.2-AC** — Module 4 Simulator missing answer-choice authorship.\\n3. **OQ-DRL-3.6-AI** — Drill 3.6 v1 evaluation strategy (templated similarity vs local sentence embeddings).\\n4. **OQ-DRL-3.9-OCR** — Drill 3.9 fashion-themed source: re-OCR or re-author.\\n5. **OQ-RR-MOBILE** — R&R Drill mobile fallback exact shape.\\n6. **OQ-CALIBRATION-CONTENT** — M1.13 + M5.8 capstone calibration content sourcing.\\n\\nRecommendations for each are inline in `CONTENT_INVENTORY.json` → `open_questions[]`.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Gate 2 → Gate 3 hand-off\\n\\nIf Joshua approves Gate 2:\\n1. A skill only runs when it\'s *activated* — **copied** (not symlinked, since 2026-07-17) into a project\'s `.claude/skills/` or `.agents/skills/` (or into the conservative global roots). This directory is search-and-storage, not a load path. A copy doesn\'t auto-follow edits to the DB source — re-copy after editing a skill here; `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` flags any activation whose `SKILL.md` has drifted.\\r\\n- **Split by tool origin, always.** Skills are filed under `claude/` or `codex/` depending on which tool\'s root they came from — **never merged into one shared folder, even when the content is byte-identical.** If a skill exists for both tools, it gets one entry under each: `claude/<name>/` and `codex/<name>/`. This keeps each tool\'s set independently editable without silently forking a shared file.\\r\\n- **A+B for vendor/plugin skills.** A vendor or plugin skill gets (A) a live symlink under `claude/<name>/` or `codex/<name>/` pointing at its real location in the plugin, so it always reflects the installed version, **and** (B) a frozen copy under `claude/_snapshots/<name>/` or `codex/_snapshots/<name>/` for point-in-time reference. Snapshots are refreshed manually, not on a schedule — re-run the refresh only when asked.\\r\\n- **Authored and project skills** are real files, source of truth, filed the same way under `claude/<name>/` or `codex/<name>/`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Save-to-both\\r\\n\\r\\nCreating a new skill (via skill-creator or by hand) always writes to **both**: the current project\'s `.claude/skills/` (or `.agents/skills/`) **and** this DB, every time, unconditionally. New skills are project-scoped by default — never auto-promoted to global.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Finding things\\r\\n\\r\\n- `claude/README.md` and `codex/README.md` index that tool\'s skills.\\r\\n- `INDEX.md` (once generated) is the full one-line-per-skill index across both, grouped by source-type.\\r\\n\\r\\nSee [PARITY.md](PARITY.md) for runtime decisions and [the decision log](../decisions/log.md) for the consolidation rationale.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"0a4093","wall_time_seconds":0.3521121,"exit_code":1,"original_token_count":51,"output":"rg: C:/Users/Joshu/.codex/.skill-lock.json: The system cannot find the file specified. Info\\"],\\n    \\"competition_ecosystem\\": [\\"competition_ecosystem\\", \\"competition\\", \\"Competition Ecosystem\\"],\\n    \\"history\\": [\\"history\\", \\"History\\"],\\n    \\"market_positioning\\": [\\"market_positioning\\", \\"market\\", \\"Market Positioning\\"],\\n}\\n\\n_SKIP_KEYS = {\\"_source_file\\", \\"uncertain\\"}\\n\\n\\ndef load_fields_yaml(fields_path):\\n    with fields_path.open(encoding=\\"utf-8\\") as f:\\n        data = yaml.safe_load(f)\\n    items = [\\n        (field[\\"name\\"], category[\\"category\\"], field.get(\\"required\\", False))\\n        for category in data.get(\\"field_categories\\", [])\\n        for field in category.get(\\"fields\\", [])\\n    ]\\n    all_fields = {name for name, _, _ in items}\\n    required_fields = {name for name, _, required in items if required}\\n    field_categories = {name: category for name, category, _ in items}\\n    return all_fields, required_fields, field_categories\\n\\n\\ndef extract_json_fields(data, category_mapping=None):\\n    category_mapping = CATEGORY_MAPPING if category_mapping is None else category_mapping\\n    nested_keys = {k for keys in category_mapping.values() for k in keys}\\n    fields = set()\\n    stack = [(data, True)]\\n    while stack:\\n        obj, is_category_level = stack.pop()\\n        if isinstance(obj, dict):\\n            for k, v in obj.items():\\n                if k in _SKIP_KEYS:\\n                    continue\\n                if is_category_level and k in nested_keys:\\n                    if isinstance(v, dict):\\n                        stack.append((v, True))\\n                    continue\\n                fields.add(k)\\n        elif isinstance(obj, list):\\n            stack.extend((item, is_category_level) for item in obj if isinstance(item, dict))\\n    return fields\\n\\n\\ndef validate_json(json_path, all_fields, required_fields, field_categories):\\n    with json_path.open(encoding=\\"utf-8\\") as f:\\n        data = json.load(f)\\n    json_fields = extract_json_fields(data)\\n    covered = all_fields & json_fields\\n    missing = all_fields - json_fields\\n    extra = json_fields - all_fields\\n    missing_required = missing & required_fields\\n    missing_by_category = defaultdict(list)\\n    for field in missing:\\n        missing_by_category[field_categories.get(field, \\"Unknown\\")].append(field)\\n    return {\\n        \\"file\\": json_path.name,\\n        \\"total_defined\\": len(all_fields),\\n        \\"covered\\": len(covered),\\n        \\"missing\\": len(missing),\\n        \\"extra\\": len(extra),\\n        \\"coverage_rate\\": len(covered) / len(all_fields) * 100 if all_fields else 100,\\n        \\"missing_required\\": sorted(missing_required),\\n        \\"missing_optional\\": sorted(missing - required_fields),\\n        \\"missing_by_category\\": {k: sorted(v) for k, v in missing_by_category.items()},\\n        \\"extra_fields\\": sorted(extra),\\n        \\"valid\\": len(missing_required) == 0,\\n    }\\n\\n\\ndef print_result(result, verbose=True):\\n    status = \\"PASS\\" if result[\\"valid\\"] else \\"FAIL\\"\\n    line = \\"=\\" * 60\\n    print(f\\"\\\\n{line}\\")\\n    print(f\\"[{status}] {result[\'file\']}\\")\\n    print(line)\\n    print(f\\"Coverage: {result[\'coverage_rate\']:.1f}% ({result[\'covered\']}/{result[\'total_defined\']})\\")\\n    if result[\\"missing_required\\"]:\\n        print(f\\"\\\\n[ERROR] Missing required fields ({len(result[\'missing_required\'])}):\\")\\n        print(\\"\\\\n\\".join(f\\"  - {f}\\" for f in result[\\"missing_required\\"]))\\n    if verbose and result[\\"missing_optional\\"]:\\n        missing_required = set(result[\\"missing_required\\"])\\n        print(f\\"\\\\n[WARN] Missing optional fields ({len(result[\'missing_optional\'])}):\\")\\n        for cat in sorted(result[\\"missing_by_category\\"]):\\n            optional = [f for f in result[\\"missing_by_category\\"][cat] if f not in missing_required]\\n            if optional:\\n                print(f\\"  [{cat}]: {\', \'.join(optional)}\\")\\n    if verbose and result[\\"extra_fields\\"]:\\n        extra = result[\\"extra_fields\\"]\\n        print(f\\"\\\\n[INFO] Extra fields ({len(extra)}):\\")\\n        print(f\\"  {\', \'.join(extra[:10])}\\")\\n        if len(extra) > 10:\\n            print(f\\"  ... and {len(extra) - 10} more\\")\\n\\n\\ndef main():\\n    import argparse\\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=\\"Validate whether JSON files cover all fields defined in fields.yaml\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\"--fields\\", \\"-f\\", type=str, help=\\"Path to fields.yaml\\", default=\\"fields.yaml\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\"--json\\", \\"-j\\", type=str, nargs=\\"*\\", help=\\"JSON file paths to validate\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\"--dir\\", \\"-d\\", type=str, help=\\"Directory containing JSON files\\", default=\\"results\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\"--quiet\\", \\"-q\\", action=\\"store_true\\", help=\\"Show summary only\\")\\n    args = parser.parse_args()\\n    fields_path = Path(args.fields)\\n    if not fields_path.exists():\\n        for p in (Path.cwd() / \\"fields.yaml\\", Path.cwd().parent / \\"fields.yaml\\"):\\n            if p.exists():\\n                fields_path = p\\n                break\\n    if not fields_path.exists():\\n        print(f\\"[ERROR] fields.yaml not found: {fields_path}\\")\\n        sys.exit(1)\\n    print(f\\"Field definition file: {fields_path}\\")\\n    all_fields, required_fields, field_categories = load_fields_yaml(fields_path)\\n    print(f\\"Total fields: {len(all_fields)} (required: {len(required_fields)}, optional: {len(all_fields) - len(required_fields)})\\")\\n    json_files = (\\n        [Path(p) for p in args.json]\\n        if args.json\\n        else sorted(Path(args.dir).glob(\\"*.json\\")) if Path(args.dir).exists() else []\\n    )\\n    if not json_files:\\n        print(\\"[WARN] No JSON files found\\")\\n        sys.exit(0)\\n    results = []\\n    for json_path in json_files:\\n        if not json_path.exists():\\n            print(f\\"[WARN] File not found: {json_path}\\")\\n            continue\\n        result = validate_json(json_path, all_fields, required_fields, field_categories)\\n        results.append(result)\\n        print_result(result, verbose=not args.quiet)\\n    line = \\"=\\" * 60\\n    print(f\\"\\\\n{line}\\")\\n    print(\\"Summary\\")\\n    print(line)\\n    passed = sum(1 for r in results if r[\\"valid\\"])\\n    avg_coverage = sum(r[\\"coverage_rate\\"] for r in results) / len(results) if results else 0\\n    print(f\\"Validation passed: {passed}/{len(results)}\\")\\n    print(f\\"Average coverage: {avg_coverage:.1f}%\\")\\n    if passed < len(results):\\n        sys.exit(1)\\n\\n\\nif __name__ == \\"__main__\\":\\n    main()\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script running with cell ID 9\nWall time 31.0 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"7406bc","wall_time_seconds":2.1614203,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":12,"output":"\\r\\nskills/codex/research-add-fields/SKILL.md\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"12df53","wall_time_seconds":0.8663322,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":3027,"output":"# Reference Study — Gate 1 Output\\r\\n\\r\\n**Status:** Draft. Pending Joshua\'s sign-off.\\r\\n**Author:** Claude Code, 2026-04-30.\\r\\n**Per:** Handoff §3.B + §6A.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor each reference: **what to study**, **what to borrow**, **what to reject**, **where it lands in the bootcamp**. Findings here feed Gate 3 architecture, never replace it (handoff §6A Rule 6).\\r\\n\\r\\n> **A note on access.** I have not loaded the live sites/apps in this session. The notes below are anchored in established product behavior across these tools (well-documented in their public-facing docs and design write-ups). Before Gate 3, I\'ll cross-reference the deployed Causation Station and Abstraction URLs (need URLs from Joshua — see Open Question #4) and revise any line below that turns out wrong on contact.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## 1. Linear (linear.app)\\r\\n\\r\\n### Study\\r\\n- Three-zone shell: persistent left rail (workspace nav, projects, views, filters), main canvas (issue list / triage / cycle), context-aware right panel (issue detail, sub-issue tree, audit log).\\r\\n- Command palette (Cmd+K) is a first-class navigation primitive. Every action reachable via keyboard.\\r\\n- Issue detail loads in-place (right drawer) without losing list context. ESC closes.\\r\\n- Density: status pills, label chips, assignee avatars all in tight rows. Mono for IDs (`ENG-1234`).\\r\\n- Motion: 150–200ms drawer slides. No fades on every state change. No bounce.\\r\\n- Status / priority / assignee are all single-keystroke editable.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Borrow\\r\\n- **Three-zone workspace shell** (handoff §6 echoes this exactly): left rail = module nav + named tools list + review queue badge + command palette trigger; canvas = active module surface; right drawer = audit / coach / reference / journal opens in place, no backdrop dim, ESC closes, 180ms slide.\\r\\n- **Command palette (Cmd+K)** for jump-to-lesson, jump-to-drill, jump-to-named-tool, jump-to-question by ID. Supports source-backed slot IDs (`MC-LSN-1.7`, `MC-DRL-3.4`, `MC-Q-PT58-S1-Q13`) — a hard requirement from handoff §6 source-backed UI slot rule.\\r\\n- **Inline status pills** in mono for module / drill / stage state.\\r\\n- **Keyboard-first nav** with visible focus rings (2px gold offset, per spec §3.7.2).\\r\\n\\r\\n### Reject\\r\\n- Linear\'s project / cycle / sprint mental model is irrelevant. Don\'t reuse it as scaffolding for module / drill / question.\\r\\n- Linear\'s color system (cool blues/violets) — we use Aspiring Gold + dark navy + role colors instead.\\r\\n- Triage queue concept — students don\'t triage; they progress through Stage-Gates.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Lands in\\r\\n- `src/components/workspace-shell/` (three-zone container).\\r\\n- `src/components/primitives/command-palette/` (Cmd+K).\\r\\n- Module nav left rail with Stage-Gate progress + named-tools quick list.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## 2. Readwise Reader (readwise.io/read)\\r\\n\\r\\n### Study\\r\\n- Editorial typography: serif body, generous line-height (1.6–1.7), wide enough measure to read without crowding but narrow enough to keep eyes anchored.\\r\\n- Highlight + ghost annotation pattern: tap a sentence → it lifts on a soft surface; a side panel hosts the note. Multi-color highlight semantic.\\r\\n- AI ghost-reader: summary + key points pinned in a side rail without dominating the read flow.\\r\\n- \\"Focus mode\\" that fades chrome when you start scrolling, restores on hover.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Borrow\\r\\n- **Long-form serif prose for Lessons module.** Spec §3.1.2 already specifies system serif for Register 2 voice — Readwise validates the readability bar for sustained reading.\\r\\n- **Sentence-level interaction surface.** When students tap a sentence in a stimulus, it elevates; the role-color overlay (X-Ray Scan) and Coach\'s Note attach to the elevated surface. Pattern lifted from how Readwise treats highlights.\\r\\n- **Side-rail context that doesn\'t dominate.** Right drawer for Coach\'s Note keeps the stimulus center-stage. Same logic as Readwise\'s ghost-reader.\\r\\n- **Focus mode** is already in spec §10 (lifted from LR Field Manual prototype) — Readwise reinforces the pattern.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Reject\\r\\n- Readwise\'s social/sharing layer (highlights → tweets / quotes export): not relevant to a focused study tool.\\r\\n- \\"Daily review\\" cards as the home surface — our home is module progress, not snippet-of-the-day.\\r\\n- Heavy reliance on imported web content metadata (titles, authors, source domain) — our content is canonical and authored.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Lands in\\r\\n- `src/modules/lessons/` typography and reading surface.\\r\\n- `src/components/x-ray-scan/` sentence-tap → role overlay interaction.\\r\\n- Right drawer\'s Coach\'s Note pane.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## 3. Superhuman (superhuman.com)\\r\\n\\r\\n### Study\\r\\n- Latency budget: every action sub-100ms perceived. Optimistic UI everywhere.\\r\\n- Single-letter keyboard shortcuts (E archive, U mark unread, etc.) with persistent cheat sheet.\\r\\n- Snippets and command palette dominate; mouse is optional.\\r\\n- Text-dense rows with mono IDs and color chips; rest of UI restrained.\\r\\n- Motion is minimal; transitions feel instant.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Borrow\\r\\n- **Latency budget as a non-functional requirement.** Drill state transitions, Stage-Gate unlocks, Indicator chip toggles must feel instant. No spinners on local-only ops. Optimistic write to persistence, reconcile on read.\\r\\n- **Single-letter shortcuts** in the Question Simulator: A–E to select, Enter to submit, R to reveal Coach\'s Note, T to open Trap Master overlay, J/K for prev/next question. Cheat sheet behind `?`.\\r\\n- **Restrained motion.** 150–220ms, eased, no celebration animations on correct answers (per handoff §1 quality bar — \\"decorative animations rejected\\").\\r\\n\\r\\n### Reject\\r\\n- Superhuman\'s split-inbox / VIP / triage paradigm — not a workflow we have.\\r\\n- Aggressive onboarding gamification (achievement unlocks, streaks). Handoff §8 explicitly rejects gamification.\\r\\n- Sound effects on send / archive — silence is gold for a study tool.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Lands in\\r\\n- Performance budget written into the build (sub-100ms for any local action).\\r\\n- `src/modules/simulator/` keyboard map.\\r\\n- `src/components/primitives/keyboard-cheat-sheet/`.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## 4. Anki (apps.ankiweb.net)\\r\\n\\r\\n### Study\\r\\n- SuperMemo SM-2 spaced-repetition algorithm: per-card ease factor, interval, last-reviewed timestamp.\\r\\n- Self-grading after each card: Again / Hard / Good / Easy. Grades feed the algorithm.\\r\\n- Per-card history (review streak, lapse count).\\r\\n- Decks ⊃ cards; subdecks for organization.\\r\\n- Browser surface for editing, tagging, filtering cards.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Borrow\\r\\n- **SM-2-based spaced repetition for Module 6.** Handoff §6 specifies \\"spaced repetition seeded from end-of-module misses\\" and `srs.queue` is in the persistence record list. SM-2 (or SM-2 + FSRS as a v1.5 upgrade) is the proven baseline.\\r\\n- **Self-grading after the answer + Coach\'s Note.** \\"Did you actually get this for the right reason?\\" with Again / Hard / Good / Easy. Pedagogical flow ruleset (post-instruction calibration) requires the student\'s honest self-assessment to feed the diagnostic engine; trap-tag-only data isn\'t enough.\\r\\n- **Per-question review history** (last seen, ease factor, lapse count) stored in `mistakes.profile` and `srs.queue`.\\r\\n- **Tag-based filtering** of practice queue: by trait number, by lesson, by indicator category, by drill type.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Reject\\r\\n- Anki\'s UI density (it\'s information-rich but visually 2005). We match Linear / Readwise polish.\\r\\n- Anki\'s \\"create your own deck\\" model — our content is canonical, students don\'t author cards.\\r\\n- Anki\'s leech-suspension mechanic — too punishing for an exam-prep tool. Use weakness profile callout instead.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Lands in\\r\\n- `src/modules/diagnostics/srs.ts` SM-2 implementation.\\r\\n- `persistence/records.ts` `SrsQueueItem` shape (id, ease, interval, due, lapses, last_grade).\\r\\n- Self-grade prompt in Question Simulator and end-of-module calibration drill.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## 5. UWorld\\r\\n\\r\\n### Study\\r\\n- Per-question full explanation: correct answer breakdown + each wrong answer addressed individually with the specific misconception that makes it tempting.\\r\\n- \\"Educational objective\\" stated above the explanation — what concept this question tests.\\r\\n- Performance dashboard slices by topic, sub-topic, percentile, time-of-day.\\r\\n- Test mode (timed, sequential) vs Tutor mode (per-question feedback) toggle.\\r\\n- Reference panel inline-accessible from any question (drug formulary, tables, etc.).\\r\\n\\r\\n### Borrow\\r\\n- **Coach\'s Note structure** (handoff §6 + spec §3.2.3) is exactly UWorld\'s per-answer audit pattern: Structure map → Core move → Per-answer audit (A/B/C/D/E with the trap each represents). Our masterclass workbooks already author this format verbatim — UWorld validates the pedagogy.\\r\\n- **Educational-objective callout** above the Coach\'s Note: \\"This question tests Trait 5 (author would agree but isn\'t the conclusion).\\" Anchors the explanation in the named-tool taxonomy.\\r\\n- **Tutor mode default for v1; timed Test mode is a toggle.** Pedagogical flow forbids cold-testing; tutor mode aligns. Test mode is for v1.5 calibration after instruction is well underway.\\r\\n- **Per-answer-choice trap tagging surfaces in analytics** (Mistake Profile dashboard slice by trait).\\r\\n- **Inline reference panel** = our right-drawer Reference Vault opening from any drill / simulator question.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Reject\\r\\n- UWorld\'s pricing surfaces and marketing chrome — not relevant to a private bootcamp.\\r\\n- \\"Question of the day\\" home surface — pedagogical flow rejects pre-test cold prompts.\\r\\n- The \\"compare your performance to the user base\\" percentile rank — vanity metric (handoff §1 explicitly rejects vanity metrics). Show absolute progress against the corpus instead.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Lands in\\r\\n- `src/components/coachs-note/CoachsNote.tsx` structure.\\r\\n- `src/components/trap-master/TraitTag.tsx` inline trait callout.\\r\\n- `src/modules/diagnostics/MistakeProfile.tsx` trait-sliced dashboard.\\r\\n- Right drawer\'s Reference panel.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## 6. Causation Station (LSAT U internal benchmark)\\r\\n\\r\\n### Study\\r\\n*(Will require deployed URL from Joshua — see Open Question #4. Notes below are based on the handoff\'s framing of it as the visual / pedagogical quality bar.)*\\r\\n- Premium private-academy aesthetic: dark mode, restrained gold accent, editorial typography.\\r\\n- Per the handoff §1, this is one of the two internal apps we must match or beat in: pedagogical depth, interaction quality, visual polish, analytics integration.\\r\\n- Likely (to verify): named-tool callouts, spec-specific drill mechanics for causation flaw types, post-question audit format.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Borrow\\r\\n- **Quality bar.** Causation Station + Abstraction define \\"premium private-academy bootcamp\\" for v1. Every screen is reviewed against this bar at Gate 4.\\r\\n- **Visual identity continuity.** If Causation Station is dark navy + Aspiring Gold + JetBrains Mono + serif prose, we match exactly to make absorption a visual layer-swap.\\r\\n- **Named-tool callout treatment.** Whatever Causation Station does for its tool taxonomy (Causal Stack, Reverse-Causation Trap, etc.), Main Conclusion mirrors for FABS / 2-Part Check / Trap Master / etc.\\r\\n- **Analytics dashboard structure.** If Causation Station already has a mistake profile / weakness viewer, Main Conclusion\'s Module 6 should resemble it for cohesion across LSAT U topics.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Reject\\r\\n- *To be determined on inspection.* Anything that feels like \\"edtech generic\\" rather than \\"private academy\\" — same rejection criteria as everywhere.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Lands in\\r\\n- Visual identity sanity-check at Gate 3.\\r\\n- Module 6 diagnostics layout reference at Gate 4.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## 7. Abstraction (LSAT U internal benchmark)\\r\\n\\r\\n### Study\\r\\n*(Same caveat as Causation Station — needs URL.)*\\r\\n- Per the handoff §6 candidate Lessons module: \\"Progressive teaching modeled on Abstraction. Intro screens, reveal sequence, inline guided examples, typed student-response checkpoints, coach translation, named-tool callouts, voice-led prose.\\"\\r\\n- This is the reference for Module 1 (Lessons) specifically.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Borrow\\r\\n- **Lessons module flow** mirrors Abstraction: intro reveal → guided example → checkpoint → coach translation → named-tool callout → voice-led prose carries student forward.\\r\\n"}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script running with cell ID 12\nWall time 31.1 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"e0ce87","wall_time_seconds":1.2158669,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2806,"output":"---\\r\\ntype: synthesis\\r\\nsubject: LSAT teaching voice standard\\r\\ncreated: 2026-10-02\\r\\nupdated: 2026-10-03\\r\\naliases: [LSAT voice reference, Conditional transcription writing standard]\\r\\ntags: [lsat-u, voice, pedagogy, writing]\\r\\nsources:\\r\\n  - ../wiki/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice.md\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# LSAT teaching: make the reasoning sound like someone thinking\\r\\n\\r\\n**This is Joshua\'s selected reference for writing LSAT teaching material.** Use it for bootcamp lessons, lecture notes, explanations, guided examples, drills, flashcards, quizzes, reference pages, and instructional feedback. It develops the teaching-content register in [the canonical teaching voice](../wiki/teaching-voice.md). That document still governs correspondence and marketing in their own registers.\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua selected the supplied **Conditional Reasoning** transcription on 2026-10-02 because of its writing, tone, word choice, cadence, point of view, progression, and depth. The goal is to reproduce those qualities closely through original wording and examples. The central quality is patient, conversational reasoning that the reader can follow and eventually perform.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe source is a calibration example. This guide separates **observed features** from **instructions for our material**. A source habit is not automatically a rule, a research finding, or permission to import a topic. Matching the voice does not require repeating the same metaphors, anecdotes, exercise sentences, or personal claims.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Find the part you need\\r\\n\\r\\n- [The evidence and coverage](#the-evidence-and-coverage)\\r\\n- [The voice in one original passage](#the-voice-in-one-original-passage)\\r\\n- [How the teacher relates to the reader](#how-the-teacher-relates-to-the-reader)\\r\\n- [Sentence length and spoken cadence](#sentence-length-and-spoken-cadence)\\r\\n- [Word choice and useful verbal habits](#word-choice-and-useful-verbal-habits)\\r\\n- [Questions that make the reader think](#questions-that-make-the-reader-think)\\r\\n- [Transitions, repetition, metaphor, and emphasis](#transitions-explain-why-the-next-move-follows)\\r\\n- [The complete source progression and depth](#the-complete-source-progression-and-depth)\\r\\n- [What to carry into the Conditional Bootcamp](#what-to-carry-into-the-conditional-bootcamp)\\r\\n- [Accuracy and method boundaries](#accuracy-and-method-boundaries)\\r\\n- [Calibration examples and review](#calibration-examples-for-different-lsat-surfaces)\\r\\n- [A reusable writing brief](#a-reusable-writing-brief)\\r\\n\\r\\n## The evidence and coverage\\r\\n\\r\\nThe [source summary](../wiki/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice.md) identifies the [preserved HTML](../raw/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice-standard/conditional_reasoning_transcription.html), [provenance](../raw/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice-standard/PROVENANCE.md), and SHA-256. Every supplied section, FAQ entry, table, worked diagram, and drill item was read. HTML line references below refer to that unchanged archived file.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe document has six main sections and six subordinate headings. It ends with six basic drill items and an answer-key reference. One line is explicitly marked partially obscured. The answer key is not supplied. This analysis covers the entire attachment; it cannot reconstruct the missing line or establish the contents of the larger chapter.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe HTML says its formatting was adjusted. We can examine what a pause, table, side note, or separated example does for learning. We cannot claim its font, spacing, color, or CSS exactly reproduces the original publication. No author is identified in the file. The exercise wording attributed to Shakespeare is separate from the narrator\'s teaching voice.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The voice in one original passage\\r\\n\\r\\nThe following is a new calibration passage. Its example was created for this guide; it is not a source quotation or an approved bootcamp page.\\r\\n\\r\\n> A conditional tells us that if one thing is true, something else has to be true too.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Suppose a shop has this rule: **Every parcel with a blue sticker has been inspected.**\\r\\n>\\r\\n> You pick up a parcel with a blue sticker. What do you know about it?\\r\\n>\\r\\n> It has been inspected. The rule covers every parcel with that sticker, including the one in your hand. A blue-sticker parcel that hadn\'t been inspected would break the rule.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Now turn the parcel over. It has an inspection stamp, but you can\'t see a blue sticker. Does it have to have one?\\r\\n>\\r\\n> We don\'t know. The rule tells us something about parcels with blue stickers. It doesn\'t tell us that every inspected parcel gets a sticker.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> That\'s the distinction we need: **what the sentence makes certain, and what it leaves open.**\\r\\n\\r\\nNotice the opening definition before the example, the small prediction, the explanation connecting the rule to the case, and the change in what the reader is given. The last sentence names a distinction the reader has just seen. The passage has a conversational rhythm because it follows a person\'s thoughts. Casual vocabulary alone would not accomplish that.\\r\\n\\r\\nIn an interactive lesson, pause after the first question and again after the inspection-stamp question. Reveal each explanation after the learner has had an opportunity to answer. The printed passage shows the whole progression for calibration.\\r\\n\\r\\n## How the teacher relates to the reader\\r\\n\\r\\n### The reader is beside the teacher\\r\\n\\r\\nThe narrator talks directly to someone doing the work. “Let\'s,” “you,” and “we” keep the reader involved. The writing seldom steps back to describe what students generally do. It asks this reader to notice, try, decide, or check something now.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe reader is treated as capable of understanding ordinary meaning. The teacher supplies support when a technical step is new. In the opening, the source first gives the literal relationship, then asks the reader to take its certainty seriously. Later, it encourages a guess before revealing the contrapositive. These are expectations of participation, not claims that everything should feel easy.\\r\\n\\r\\n**For our writing:** speak to the reader without making their intelligence the subject. Do not copy a line such as “Because you\'re a thinking human.” Give them a manageable question and the information to answer it. If they miss it, explain the precise move that went wrong.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Confidence lives in the reasoning\\r\\n\\r\\nThe source is decisive about a valid consequence. It also says “We don\'t know” when the information cannot settle a question. These two kinds of confidence belong together. Clear teaching can be certain about the limits of what is known.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe teacher does not apologize for asking the reader to think. The explanation makes the request reasonable. In the FAQ, the narrator acknowledges early mistakes and explains the value of practicing anyway. The tone combines patience with a specific demand: attempt the question, write the diagram, inspect the error.\\r\\n\\r\\n**For our writing:** state the consequence plainly and show why. Preserve legitimate uncertainty. Avoid changing a logical “could” into “must” to make the prose sound stronger. Avoid guarantees about learning outcomes, timing, or score changes unless we have evidence.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Warmth points at the work\\r\\n\\r\\nEncouragement is usually tied to an action. The reader can look back at the lesson, make a provisional attempt, learn from a wrong translation, or build confidence by checking work. The warmth comes from useful help and a teacher who expects progress.\\r\\n\\r\\nUseful form:\\r\\n\\r\\n> If the direction felt unclear, read the statement back in ordinary English. What does it say you need? Keep that complete idea in your diagram.\\r\\n\\r\\nWeak form:\\r\\n\\r\\n> You\'ve got this! Conditionals are easy once you know the trick!\\r\\n\\r\\nThe weak version offers reassurance without a way forward and minimizes the actual difficulty.\\r\\n\\r\\n### “I,” “we,” and “you” have different jobs\\r\\n\\r\\n| Pronoun | What it does in the source | Use in our material |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| **You** | Gives the reader an action or asks for a decision. | “Try the sentence in the other direction. Does it still say the same thing?” |\\r\\n| **We / let\'s** | Makes the explanation a shared investigation. | “Let\'s keep the rule and change the fact.” |\\r\\n| **I**, thinking aloud | Shows the teacher making a decision: noticing an indicator, identifying its target, placing a label. | “I see ‘requires.’ What does it say is required?” |\\r\\n| **I**, hypothetical | Places the speaker inside a condition. | “If I\'m taking this course, what does the sentence say I need?” |\\r\\n| **I**, personal judgment | Gives a recommendation or reports experience. | Use only a genuine Joshua judgment or verified experience. Do not borrow the source narrator\'s student history. |\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep these roles stable. Do not rotate pronouns for cosmetic variety. In a platform instruction, “you” may be enough. In a teacher-authored walkthrough, “I” can make a decision visible. Marketing remains governed by its separate platform voice.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Sentence length and spoken cadence\\r\\n\\r\\n### Short sentences settle; longer sentences explain\\r\\n\\r\\nThe source does not make every sentence short. A longer sentence introduces a situation, explains motivation, or qualifies advice. Short sentences supply a result, mark a limit, or redirect attention.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe opening at HTML line 144 moves from a definition to a plain rephrasing and then the two-word landing “That\'s it.” Later, the source uses several brief sentences to think through a missing requirement, then states the consequence. The broad-purpose paragraph at line 152 is substantially longer. Its job is to explain why understanding the relationship matters across varied wording.\\r\\n\\r\\n**For our writing:** let each sentence carry a manageable thought. A difficult inference may need several sentences. Do not trim away the intermediate reason just to produce a punchy conclusion. A short landing belongs after the explanation has earned it.\\r\\n\\r\\nOriginal rhythm:\\r\\n\\r\\n> The inspection stamp tells us the parcel was inspected. It doesn\'t tell us which sticker the shop gave it afterward. The rule hasn\'t answered that question.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe first sentence identifies what is known. The second identifies the missing connection. The third closes the uncertainty.\\r\\n\\r\\n### The observed numbers are calibration, not quotas\\r\\n\\r\\nAn [approximate prose analysis](../raw/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice-standard/analysis-metrics.json) selects unclassified HTML paragraph elements, excluding the drill and obscured-line marker. It includes prose inside notes and FAQ answers, but excludes displayed examples, table cells, headings, and many short callouts. Words are alphanumeric tokens with internal apostrophes; sentences are split after `.`, `!`, or `?` followed by whitespace. missing:\n+        missing_by_category[field_categories[field]].append(field)\n+    errors = []\n+    if missing & required_fields:\n+        errors.append(\"Missing required fields: \" + \", \".join(sorted(missing & required_fields)))\n+    if require_complete:\n+        if missing:\n+            errors.append(\"Incomplete field coverage: \" + \", \".join(sorted(missing)))\n+        if extra:\n+            errors.append(\"Unknown fields: \" + \", \".join(sorted(extra)))\n+        uncertain = data.get(\"uncertain\")\n+        if not isinstance(uncertain, list) or not all(isinstance(item, str) for item in uncertain):\n+            errors.append(\"uncertain must be an array of field names, including [] when none.\")\n+        else:\n+            if len(uncertain) != len(set(uncertain)):\n+                errors.append(\"uncertain contains duplicate field names.\")\n+            if set(uncertain) - all_fields:\n+                errors.append(\"uncertain contains unknown field names.\")\n+            marked = {name for name, value in values.items() if contains_marker(value)}\n+            if set(uncertain) != marked:\n+                errors.append(\"uncertain must exactly match fields containing [uncertain].\")\n+    return {\n+        \"file\": json_path.name, \"total_defined\": len(all_fields),\n+        \"covered\": len(all_fields & json_fields), \"missing\": len(missing), \"extra\": len(extra),\n+        \"coverage_rate\": len(all_fields & json_fields) / len(all_fields) * 100,\n+        \"missing_required\": sorted(missing & required_fields),\n+        \"missing_optional\": sorted(missing - required_fields),\n+        \"missing_by_category\": {key: sorted(value) for key, value in missing_by_category.items()},\n+        \"extra_fields\": sorted(extra), \"errors\": errors, \"valid\": not errors,\n+    }\n+\n+\n+def main(argv=None):\n+    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)\n+    parser.add_argument(\"--fields\", \"-f\", default=\"fields.yaml\", type=Path)\n+    source = parser.add_mutually_exclusive_group()\n+    source.add_argument(\"--json\", \"-j\", nargs=\"+\", type=Path)\n+    source.add_argument(\"--dir\", \"-d\", type=Path, default=Path(\"results\"))\n+    parser.add_argument(\"--require-complete\", action=\"store_true\")\n+    parser.add_argument(\"--quiet\", \"-q\", action=\"store_true\")\n+    args = parser.parse_args(argv)\n+    try:\n+        all_fields, required_fields, categories = load_fields_yaml(args.fields)\n+        files = args.json if args.json is not None else sorted(args.dir.glob(\"*.json\"))\n+        if not files:\n+            raise ValueError(\"No JSON inputs found; validation did not run.\")\n+        results = []\n+        for path in files:\n+            try:\n+                result = validate_json(path, all_fields, required_fields, categories, args.require_complete)\n+            except (OSError, ValueError) as exc:\n+                result = {\"file\": str(path), \"valid\": False, \"errors\": [str(exc)]}\n+            results.append(result)\n+            print(f\"[{'PASS' if result['valid'] else 'FAIL'}] {result['file']}\")\n+            for error in result[\"errors\"]:\n+                print(f\"  {error}\")\n+            if not args.quiet and \"coverage_rate\" in result:\n+                print(f\"  Coverage: {result['coverage_rate']:.1f}%\")\n+        passed = sum(item[\"valid\"] for item in results)\n+        print(f\"Validation passed: {passed}/{len(results)}\")\n+        return 0 if passed == len(results) else 1\n+    except (OSError, ValueError) as exc:\n+        print(f\"[ERROR] {exc}\", file=sys.stderr)\n+        return 1\n+    except Exception as exc:\n+        if yaml is not None and isinstance(exc, yaml.YAMLError):\n+            print(f\"[ERROR] Invalid YAML: {exc}\", file=sys.stderr)\n+            return 1\n+        raise\n+\n+\n+if __name__ == \"__main__\":\n+    sys.exit(main())\n*** End Patch"));
